   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mfrac><msup><mrow><mo>(
   </mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>λ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></msu
   p></mrow><mo>)</mo></mrow><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></msup><m
   row><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mn>2</mn><mo>+</mo><msub><mi>λ</mi><mn>0<
   /mn></msub></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow></mfrac><mo>≈</mo><mn>10.7809</mn></mrow>
   <mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">D_{{\mathbb{R}},6}(2)=D_{{\mathbb{R}},6}(F
   )=\frac{\left(2+|\lambda_{0}|^{\frac{12}{7}}\right)^{\frac{12}{7}}}{|2+
   \lambda_{0}|}\thickapprox 10.7809.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   In any case we do have that
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>D</mi><mrow><mi>ℝ</mi><mo>,</mo>
   <mn>6</mn></mrow></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mn>10.7809</mn></mrow><
   mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">D_{{\mathbb{R}},6}(2)\geq
   10.7809.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   Refer to caption Figure 3. Graph of the quotient
   [MATH: <semantics><mfrac><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</m
   n><mo>,</mo><mi>b</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>0</mn><mo>,</mo><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo
   ><mn>0</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mfrac><mn>12</mn><mn>7</mn></mfrac></msu
   b><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>Q</mi><mrow><mi>a</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>b<
   /mi></mrow></msub><mo stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow></mfrac><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\frac{|(0,a,0,b,0,a,0)|_{\frac{12}{7}}}{\|
   Q_{a,b}\|}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   as a function of
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>λ</mi><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mi>b</mi><mi>a</mi></mfrac
   ></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\lambda=\frac{b}{a}</annotation></semantic
   s> :MATH]
   .

   As we did in the previous cases, it would be interesting to know if we
   can improve numerically our best lower bound on
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>H</mi><mrow><mi>ℝ</mi><mo>,</mo><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi></mrow></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">H_{{\mathbb{R}},\infty}</annotation></sema
   ntics> :MATH]
   by considering powers of
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><msub><mi>Q</mi><m
   row><mn>1</mn><mo>,</mo><msub><mi>λ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub></mrow></msub>
   <mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>y</mi><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>=</mo><mrow><mrow><msup><mi>x<
   /mi><mn>5</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>y</mi></mrow><mo>+</mo><mrow><msub><mi>λ</mi><mn
   >0</mn></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>3</mn></msup></mrow><mo>+</mo><m
   row><mi>x</mi><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><msup><mi>y</mi><mn>5</mn></msup></mrow></mrow></mro
   w><mo>,</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">P_{6}(x,y)=Q_{1,\lambda_{0}}(x,y)=x^{5}y+\
   lambda_{0}x^{3}y^{3}+xy^{5},</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   with
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>λ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\lambda_{0}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   as in Theorem 3.6 (
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><msub><mi>λ</mi><mn>0</mn></msub><mo
   rspace="0em">≈</mo><mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">−</mo><mn>2.2654</mn></mrow></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\lambda_{0}\approx-2.2654</annotation></se
   mantics> :MATH]
   ). If
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>𝐚</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbf{a}_{n}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   is the vector of the coefficients of
   [MATH:
   <semantics><msubsup><mi>P</mi><mn>6</mn><mi>n</mi></msubsup><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">P_{6}^{n}</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   for each
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>∈</mo><mi>ℕ</mi></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">n\in{\mathbb{N}}</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]
   , then we know that
   (3.4)
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>D</mi><mrow><mi>ℝ</mi><mo>,</mo>
   <mrow><mn>6</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow></mrow></msub><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mfrac><msub><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo><msub><mi>𝐚</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">|</mo></mrow><mfrac><mrow><mn>12</mn><mo lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mrow><mrow><mn>6</mn><mo
   lspace="0em"
   rspace="0em">​</mo><mi>n</mi></mrow><mo>+</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow></mfrac>
   </msub><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><mi>n</mi></msup></mfrac></mrow><mo
   lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">D_{{\mathbb{R}},6n}(2)\geq\frac{|\mathbf{a
   }_{n}|_{\frac{12n}{6n+1}}}{\|P_{6}\|^{n}}.</annotation></semantics>
   :MATH]

   Using (3.4) with
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">n=100</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   and estimating
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo><msub><mi>P</mi><mn>6</mn></msub><mo
   stretchy="false">‖</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">\|P_{6}\|</annotation></semantics> :MATH]
   according to Remark 3.1 we obtain
   [MATH:
   <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>D</mi><mrow><mi>ℝ</mi><mo>,</mo>
   <mn>600</mn></mrow></msub><mo lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><msup><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1.58432</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><mn>600</mn></msup></mrow><mo>,</mo></mro
   w><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">D_{\mathbb{R},600}(2)\geq(1.58432)^{600},<
   /annotation></semantics> :MATH]

   which suggests that (see Figure 4)
   [MATH: <semantics><mrow><mrow><mrow><msub><mi>H</mi><mrow><mi
   mathvariant="normal">∞</mi><mo>,</mo><mi>ℝ</mi></mrow></msub><mo
   lspace="0em" rspace="0em">​</mo><mrow><mo
   stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo
   stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><mo>≥</mo><mn>1.58432</mn></mrow><
   mo lspace="0em">.</mo></mrow><annotation
   encoding="application/x-tex">H_{\infty,\mathbb{R}}(2)\geq
   1.58432.</annotation></semantics> :MATH]

3.7. Numerical calculation of
[MATH:
<semantics><mrow><msub><mi>D</mi><mrow><mi>ℝ</mi><mo>,</mo><mn>10</mn></mrow>

stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mo
stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow></mrow><annotation
encoding="application/x-tex">D_{{\mathbb{R}},10}(2)</annotation></semantics>
:MATH]

   In this case our numerical estimates show that there exists an extreme
   polynomial in the Bohnenblust-Hille polynomial inequality in

   of the form

   If
   [MATH: <semantics><msub><mi>𝐚</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><annotation

   :MATH]
   is the vector of the coefficients of

   , then we know that
   (3.7)

   If we set
